### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

3Q26 review: margin likely bottomed-out

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups   
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from relatedparties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Win Semi’s 3Q26 core business (operating income) was 49/41% 
higher than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by a more favorable 
product mix with higher contribution from the high margin 
infrastructure business (accounted for 39% of total revenue in 3Q26 
vs. 34% in 2Q26), resulting in a 9.2ppt QoQ increase in 3Q26 GPM, 
and came in 6.9/6.1ppt above GSe/BBG consensus. Win Semi’s 
OPEX increased to NT$775mn (vs. NT$743mn in 2Q26). NI was 
67/110% higher than GSe/BBG consensus due to the high 
non-operating income of NT$698mn (account for 33% of total 
pretax income). 
For 4Q26, the company expects revenue to increase by low single 
digit QoQ, and expects cellular to see QoQ decline due to weak 
season while the remaining businesses (including 
infrastructure/Wi-Fi/optical) will see flattish to small QoQ increase. 
For GM, the company guides the 4Q26 level to maintain at 
high-thirties %, which is much better than both our prior 
expectation and BBG consensus at 31.8/31.4%. Management 
indicated 3Q26 UTR to be higher at 75% (was 65% in 2Q26), and 
expect to maintain at similar level in 4Q26.  
The company continues to highlight the progress with its AI-related 
business, with its new PD (photodiodes) products started to ramp 
up from 2H26, with more contribution expected in 2027 along with 
more PD capacity coming online. For the new LD (laser diodes) 
products, the company continues to work with multiple clients on 
multiple new solutions (including CW laser, EML solutions and 
VCSEL), but expects to see more contribution in 2028 due to the 
lengthy reliability tests (takes 6M+ per our estimates). The company 
reiterated that CAPEX this year is project driven, which will mainly 
focus on expanding InP and GaN capacity for the datacom optical 
and infrastructure business.

### Page 3

Upward revision to PA foundry TAM reflecting foundry continue to gain
share despite weak smartphone shipment
We have revised up our 2026/27/28E P A foundry TAM by 5/4/2% to
US$1.3bn/1.5bn/1.6bn despite smartphone shipment remaining weak (GSe now at
-10%/+2%/+2% in 2026/27/28, was -10%/+3%/+1% previously, see here) as the
smartphone market remains exposed to the rising memory cost environment (see here).
The upward revision is based on our expectation that PA foundry house will continue to

gain share in the smartphone PA segment as newer smartphone projects from design
houses are being allocated to foundries due to the higher production costs and
increasing design complexity of the PA. This will benefit Win Semi in the mid to long term
(Win Semi’s revenue from cellular business already saw 28% YoY YTD). We now expect
the market TAM to deliver an 8% CAGR over 2026-30E, and we expect foundries’ cellular
PA shipments to increase by 11% in 2026 (Exhibit 2), outperforming our 2026

smartphone shipment estimates of -10% YoY due to the higher than expected PA
foundry penetration (Exhibit 5).
Win Semi delivered 3Q26 revenue that is better than the company’s guidance (actual
QoQ at 31% vs. guidance of low-teens QoQ), with the cellular business in peak season.
Management expects this momentum to slow down in 4Q26 and guided revenue to see
QoQ decline in the cellular business. We now expect Win Semi’s PA foundry revenue to
grow 31% YoY in 2026 (was 28% previously), outpacing the overall PA foundry TAM

growth of 22% YoY, as the company continues to focus on mid-to-high-end and
premium smartphone models (iOS camp accounts for 30-40% of the company’s cellular
business per our estimate), which are relatively more resilient to rising memory costs.
Moreover, we expect Win Semi’s PA foundry market share to decline from 74% in 2020
to 57% in 2026, and further lower to ~50% by 2030, but its revenue will continue to
expand further due to the growing foundry TAM.

We continue to expect Taiwan PA foundry players to see modest recovery in 2026
CAPEX. However, we do not view this as an indication of a recovery in demand for the
overall PA foundry industry. Win Semi maintained its 2026 CAPEX guidance of
NT$2-3bn, with the majority of spending allocated to the new InP and GaN capacity
rather than GaAs capacity expansion. We continue to believe GaAs capacity remains
sufficient as we estimate Win Semi’s quarterly utilization rate could reach at most 85%

throughout 2026-28E (Exhibit 1), suggesting no meaningful need for additional GaAs
capacity expansion.

### Page 5

Investment view 
The company has started to see increasing contribution from the new PD product in 
3Q26 but the contribution remains very limited (overall optical business only saw high 
single digit QoQ in 3Q26), while the company expects PD product revenue to further 
ramp up in 2027 along with more PD capacity coming online. This is tracking more slowly 
than our expectation, and we now expect the datacom optical business to account for 
only a mid-single-digit of 2026E revenue (was expecting high-single-digit previously). 
Meanwhile, progress in new high end LD products (including CW lasers, EML solutions 
and VCSEL) remains broadly the same since last earning call, with the company is still at 
verification stage and expect more meaningful contribution in 2028E. As such, we 
believe Win Semi’s revenue remains highly exposed to the potential weakness in 
smartphone demand, as we estimate smartphone related business (including cellular, 
WiFi, and optical) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2026E. The contribution

### Page 6

from datacom optical through 2026-27E could not reach scale, and it remains highly
uncertain whether optical companies will outsource LD products to Win Semi in 2028E.
As a result, we believe Win Semi’s stock price remains too elevated (trading at over 50x
2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG consensus) with limited contribution from the datacom
optical business.
We maintain our conservative view on Win Semi’s stock performance, given (1) the
contribution from datacom optical products (e.g., PDs and LDs) remains limited in

near-term, with the higher margin LD products potentially seeing more contribution in
2028E, (2) the company’s exposure in cellular business remains high (30%+), and the
smartphone market continue to be impacted by high memory cost; and (3) given the
slow technology upgrade speed in the GaAs foundry market, we believe competitors
such as AWSC could catch up with the technology and is better positioned to gain share
given their lower cost structures and more competitive pricing.

Maintain Sell, with a new 12-m TP of NT$162 (from NT$142).

### Page 7

Earnings revisions  
We raise our 2026E revenue by 2% after factor in 3Q26 results and raise our 2027/28E 
revenue by 5/6% to factor in higher infrastructure and optical business contribution. We 
revise up our 2026/27/28E GM estimates by 3.8/2.1/1.6ppt as we expect more margin 
expansion driven by higher UTR and the higher contribution from high margin 
infrastructure business. Overall, we revised up our 2026/27/28E EPS estimates by 
47/6/7%. 
Also, we introduce our 2029E earnings estimates with this report.

Valuation 
We maintain our Sell rating, and revise up our TP from NT$142 to NT$162, based on the 
same target PE of 24x (1x STDV lower than the industry upcycle average valuation), 
factoring in our earnings revisions and, consistent with our group coverage practices, roll 
over our valuation period from 4Q26-3Q27 to 2027E.

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

Win Semi is a key GaAs-based semi foundry player with c.49% global market share as of 
2025. We believe the company’s market share could decline given its much 
higher-than-peer product pricing in the demand downtrend. We also expect the

company to be impacted by the slow PA foundry market growth outlook given weak
end-market demand and high inventory levels. This is despite some longer-term demand
drivers (China PA in-sourcing trend, WiFi 6E/7 upgrading trend, new smartphone/AR VR
optical products, automotive LiDAR opportunity, satellite communication market
opportunity, stronger 5G BTS deployment in EU/US market, datacom optical
opportunity etc.), which should increase its revenue/GM in the long term. However, we

believe Win Semi’s market share loss will continue to weigh on its long-term growth
opportunity. We are Sell-rated given the ongoing PA foundry industry downcycle, along
with our cautious view on the company’s market share outlook. Win Semi is now trading
above its average downcycle P/E multiple. Key upside risks are (1) easing competition in
the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade
trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related business.

Our 12-month target price of NT$162 is based on 24x 2027E EPS, which is 1x STDV
lower than the industry upcycle average valuation since 2016. Key upside risks are (1)
easing competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress
on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related
business.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

股價相對大盤走勢

長榮航太 (2645 TT)  Trading Buy

預期波音 737 系列交機速度將略為放緩。  
投資建議 ：雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量
波音將維持 737 系列月均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太
的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航太 Trading Buy 的投資建
議。  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造
的營運預估： 波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會
上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時
間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到 2H27 才會上升至 52
架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時間，故研
究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
3Q26 先前的維修漲價陸續貢獻，營運維持高檔： 3Q26 長榮航
太自 2H24 起陸續針對維修業務進行漲價後，加上全球機隊持
續供不應求，增加維修機率與維修產值，惟製造部門雖然波音
已將 737 系列交機上升至 47 架 /月，但短期拉貨力道仍有限，
使得長榮航太的製造業務與轉投資公司營運維持低檔，營運仍
以維修業務為主， 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元， QoQ+1.02% ，預估稅
後純益 8.31 億元， QoQ+10.34% ， EPS 2.22 元。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營
運新動能： 2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機
的逐年擴張之外，在新訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，
預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待後續取得特殊製程認證後，
2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單也有望成為長榮
航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ，EPS 10.04 元。

波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系
列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到
2H27 才會上升至 52 架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時
間，故研究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營運新動能：  
2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機的逐年擴張之外，在新
訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待
後續取得特殊製程認證後， 2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單
也有望成為長榮航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ， EPS 10.04 元。  
投資建議：  
雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量波音將維持 737 系列月
均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航
太 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

작성자(한승한)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: AI Jitters Continue While Fed Pauses Hiking Rates
Date:    1 day ago
AI Jitters Continue While Fed Pauses Hiking Rates

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Jitters Continue While Fed
Pauses Hiking Rates
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 29 Oct 2026
View in browser

I. The AI Boom Versus The AI Trade
AI jitters will be back tomorrow after taking a breather so far this week, as
encouraging developments surrounding the AI investment boom helped
restore confidence and lift technology stocks. The S&P 500 rose 0.3% on
Wednesday, moving back above its 50-day moving average (chart).

### Page 2

The Nasdaq-100 advanced 0.6%, recovering toward its 50-day moving
average while remaining above its 200-day moving average (chart).
Among chipmakers, Micron jumped 5.9%, AMD gained 3.2%, and
Broadcom rose 2.8% (chart). SK Hynix shares surged as much as 9.4% in
Seoul following blockbuster earnings.
Three recent developments helped revive confidence in the AI trade:
(1) More spending. $235 billion in prospective AI financing emerged this
week, including Anthropic's expected $100 billion IPO raise, Meta's plans

to raise up to $95 billion through equity issuance, and SpaceX's $40 billion
debt deal to purchase GPUs. These developments suggest capital
remains available for the AI buildout, with equity markets providing an
alternative if debt financing tightens.

### Page 3

(2) Encouraging AI economics. Anthropic's S-1 filing offered five
encouraging signals about the sustainability of compute demand. First, H1
revenues surged from $1.2 billion to $16.3 billion y/y. Second, operating
losses narrowed from $2.1 billion to $693 million, with Q2 marking its first
quarterly operating profit. Third, 11,900 customers now spend at least
$100,000 annually, up from roughly 950 a year earlier. Fourth, revenue
per dollar of compute expense increased more than fivefold to $2.08.

Fifth, Anthropic held $131.7 billion in cash and short-term investments to
finance expansion.
(3) Strong semiconductor earnings. SK Hynix's Q3 results reinforced
confidence in AI infrastructure demand. Operating profit surged more than
sevenfold y/y, while the company announced roughly $75 billion in
shareholder returns. Most encouragingly, management expects memory
shortages through 2030, with production effectively sold out through 2029,
suggesting durable demand and pricing power.

These developments reinforce our view that the AI investment boom is
here to stay. But that doesn't mean that the AI trade won't remain jittery.
This evening, Alphabet shares are down about 10% after the company
missed Q3 consensus revenues and earnings estimates. Free cash flow
was also more negative than expected, while the company remains
committed to significant capex of over $200 billion this year and $300
billion in 2027. Long-term debt rose to $125.1 billion, up from $21.6 billion

a year earlier.
There was some good news. Cloud revenue more than doubled to $30.7
billion. Google said its Gemini app reached 1 billion users, making it the
fastest-growing product in its history. Furthermore, Microsoft shares rose
4% in extended trading this evening after the company reported better-
than-expected quarterly results, driven by 48% growth in its Azure cloud
infrastructure business.
II. The Fed Pauses Before Midterm Elections
The FOMC voted 10-2 Wednesday to hold the federal funds rate at

3.75%-4.00%, with Lisa Cook and Beth Hammack favoring a 25bps hike.
Chair Kevin Warsh acknowledged robust economic growth, a healthy
labor market, stubborn inflation, and financial conditions that aren't tight.
Yet he cited tightening financial conditions, anchored inflation

### Page 4

expectations, and improving productivity as reasons for patience. Here's
more:
(1) Economic activity. Warsh observed that "economic growth has
strengthened." Consumer spending remains robust and business
investment is growing at a double-digit pace. The capex story, he said, is
"not just robust, not just strong, but it is broadening."
(2) Labor market. Unemployment is lower than a year ago, payroll growth
has improved, and initial jobless claims remain near historic lows.

Employment conditions are "quite positive," Warsh said, with FOMC
discussions focused primarily on price stability.
(3) Inflation. "Inflation is too high," Warsh acknowledged, warning that
rising energy prices could spread through the economy. He rejected a
higher implicit inflation target: "There's no soft inflation target, there's no
implicit inflation target." Still, he believes inflation expectations remain
anchored.
(4) Financial conditions. Warsh noted that tighter financial conditions are

prompting more selective capital allocation. Rejecting claims that AI
investment is insensitive to interest rates, he observed that "interest rates
get in the cracks" as credit spreads have widened for some borrowers.
Nevertheless, "while overall financial conditions are tighter, I'm unwilling
and unable to call them tight."
(5) Productivity. Warsh disputed that robust growth necessarily implies
overheating: "That isn't the judgment of most of my colleagues." Improving

productivity, much of it predating recent AI advances, may have raised
potential growth without fueling inflation.
(6) Revamping the Fed. Warsh reiterated his opposition to forward
guidance outside crises. Yet other officials' comments shifted market
expectations away from an October hike despite hotter-than-expected
September CPI and PPI inflation reports, suggesting forward guidance
remains intact. He also plans to dedicate one FOMC meeting in 2027 and

two in 2028 to monetary policy strategy.
In our view, stronger productivity growth will let the economy expand
faster without adding inflationary pressure. That could be an argument

### Page 5

against restrictive monetary policy, but not against returning to neutral,
which we estimate at 4.25%-4.50%. We agree that September's hike
“removed a dose of accommodation,” but believe another 50bps is
needed to remove the rest. The Fed doesn't need to throw sand in the
economy's gears. It just needs to take its foot off the accelerator. We
wouldn't rule out a 50bps hike in December.
💡

clause.

Document 244: AI股重挫而等权标普逆势涨1.2%，必需消费领涨并一度创新高

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI股重挫而等权标普逆势涨1.2%，必需消费领涨并一度创新高.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

瑞银的 Mark Paski ，剔除 AI 股的标普 500 成分股本⽉有望上涨 6% ，为 2022 ⾄ 2023 年 AI 交
易开始升温以来的最佳⽉度表现。随着利率⾛低、企业盈利保持韧性，上涨正从少数消费
⻰头扩散到更多股票。
摘要：
周五早盘， AI 股再遭重挫，纳斯达克 100 指数下跌 3% ，等权重标普 500 指数却逆势上涨约
1.2% 。指数之下的分化表明，跌幅⾼度集中在⼤型科技股和 AI 相关股票上。
标普 500 指数当时下跌 1.5% 。英特尔录得有记录以来最⼤⽇内跌幅， AI 主导的抛售让本周的科技
股跌势进⼀步加深；⾼盛追踪美国上市 AI 概念股的 GS Broad AI US Index 下跌 4.7% 。
防御板块则受到追捧。必需消费是标普 500 表现最好的板块，上涨 1.64% ，板块指数在纽约午盘
前⼀度创下纪录新⾼。
财报撑起必需消费
标普 1500 必需消费指数盘中最多上涨 2.3% ，临近美东时间 11 点回吐部分涨幅。领涨的是两家刚
交出亮眼财报的公司。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⾼露洁 - 棕榄盘前⼀度上涨 5.5% ，开盘后涨约 2.5% 。这家家庭及个⼈护理⽤品公司的利润和总销
售额均超过彭博⼀致预期。尽管美国及北美市场表现偏软，但已在改善，强劲的利润和业绩指
引占了上⻛。
零⻝⽣产商亿滋国际盘前涨幅略超 7% ，午盘前上涨约 3% 。公司第三季度业绩超出预期，主要靠
稳健的销量增⻓。
剔除 AI 股，标普 500 本⽉有望涨 6%
瑞银的 Mark Paski 表⽰，剔除 AI 股的标普 500 成分股本⽉有望上涨 6% ，为 2022 ⾄ 2023 年 AI 交易
开始升温以来的最佳⽉度表现。
他认为，随着利率⾛低、企业盈利保持韧性，上涨正从少数⻰头扩散到更多股票。第三季度财
报季初期， 78% 的公司每股收益超出市场预期，但利润率仍是消费类公司的关键问题。
可选消费公司对需求的判断也偏乐观。希尔顿预计，旗下酒店体系的增⻓势头将延续⾄今年余
下时间并进⼊ 2027 年；皇家加勒⽐称， 2027 年的预订进度领先于历史⽔平，价格也更⾼；捷蓝
航空则表⽰， 9 ⽉的强劲表现延续到了 10 ⽉。 Paski 提醒，更⼤的不确定性仍在利润和利润率。

“ 如果市场⼴度继续改善，除了近期的领涨股，基本⾯更强的消费类股票也会越来越具备配置价
值。 ” 他写道。
从周五的盘⾯看，投资者是在把资⾦从 AI 交易挪向防御类股票，并没有恐慌性地全⾯撤离股市。
即便 AI 相关交易遭到重创，普通个股依然站得住。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng.

trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

Document 272: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 275: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-10-31)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-10-31).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets were generally buoyed overnight, with Anthropic’s
IPO filing and Meta’s US$25bn equity offering closing five
times oversubscribed adding momentum back into the
AI trade, while softer oil prices provided further support.

Attention now turns to a series of key economic developments,
with Australian CPI due today alongside policy decisions and
accompanying guidance from the RBNZ, BoC and FOMC.
• US equities moved higher, supported by AI-related
stocks. The S&P 500 gained 0.8%, while the NASDAQ
outperformed, rising 1.2%, and the Dow Jones was
unchanged. European markets were mixed, with the Euro
Stoxx 50 up 0.8%, while the FTSE 100 fell 0.8% ahead of
the UK Budget.
• Tech-heavy Asian markets were buoyed as recent headlines

helped ease concerns around AI financing. The KOSPI led
gains, rising 3.1%, while the Nikkei 225 also performed
strongly, up 1.7%. The Hang Seng diverged from regional
peers, falling 0.7%. Locally, the ASX 200 gained 0.5%.
• US Treasury yields were little changed, with the 2-year
yield rising 1bp and the 10-year yield unchanged. Futures
markets remain cautious about pricing further Fed tightening
in 2026, with the probability of a hike at today’s FOMC

meeting sitting at just 7%, taking the implied year-end fed
funds rate to 4.10%.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed
ahead of today’s CPI release, with the 3-year yield rising
1bp and the 10-year yield falling 1bp. Swap markets remain
cautious around additional RBA tightening in 2026, with the
probability of a November rate hike priced at 62%, taking
the implied year-end cash rate to 4.80%.
• Pre-FOMC positioning supported the US dollar, with

the DXY up 0.3%. The AUD outperformed other major
currencies, rising 0.2% against the greenback to 0.7028 and
also gaining across the major crosses. The euro and yen both
fell 0.3% against the USD, while the sterling declined 0.2%.
• Oil prices declined on hopes that diplomacy could avert a
renewed escalation in the US-Iran conflict. Brent fell 1.6%
to US$114/bbl, while WTI declined 1.9% to US$99.9/bbl.
Dated Brent traded at US$121/bbl. Gold rose 1.1% ahead

of today’s FOMC meeting, while positive risk sentiment
supported base metals, with copper up 1.0% and aluminium
gaining 1.5%. Iron ore fell 1.1%, remaining below US$90/t
amid persistent concerns around global demand.
International Data
US durable goods orders rose 1.8% in September after a 0.1%
gain in August. Growth for core orders (non defence, ex aircraft)
was more modest in September at 0.5% but follows a 1.6% rise
in August.
US S&P Cotality Case Shiller house prices rose a further 0.3%

in August, accelerating annual growth at the margin to 2.7%yr.
US new home sales fell 0.6% in September following a
downwardly revised 5.1% gain (prev 6.4%) in August. Sales are
currently near the average of the past 5 years.
US Conference Board consumer confidence jumped from 84.2
to 91.7 in October on an improvement in current conditions
and expectations. While still below average, this measure of
consumer confidence continues to outperform the University of

Michigan survey, held up by the robust strength of the labour
market. Responses to the Michigan measure instead look to be
focused more on cost of living.
The Richmond Fed manufacturing index was unchanged at -2
in October.
Local Data
Our New Zealand team published their 2026 General Election
preview, outlining the major parties’ key policies (see here).

Document 370: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

Document 384: Stablecoin growth – opportunity or threat for banks_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Stablecoin growth – opportunity or threat for banks_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

While stablecoin adoption could signiﬁcantly aﬀect European
banks, including by shifting their funding base, the sector could
also beneﬁt from the technology by issuing its own tokens.
Upcoming regulatory changes could alter the impact of
stablecoins on banks, but we should not expect banks to
surrender ground to ﬁntech issuers without a ﬁght
Marine Leleux
Stablecoins are increasingly moving into the mainstream ﬁnancial system as
banks assess both the risks and opportunities they present

Stablecoins’ market capitalisation has grown signiﬁcantly over the
past two years. This growth has drawn widespread scrutiny,
including from regulators, as it raises concerns about ﬁnancial
stability. Banks have also paid closer attention to the sector as it
could represent both new risks and opportunities.
In this piece, we review the potential impact stablecoins could have
on the European banking sector. This is especially relevant as
multiple regulatory changes are expected in 2027. The EU is

reviewing its Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCAR), which

### Page 2

could aﬀect the estimated impact of stablecoins on the banking
sector. Across the Atlantic, the regulatory landscape could also
change next year. Discussions could result in the US allowing
stablecoin issuers to directly distribute rewards, which would be a
ﬁrst.
Overall, while the future of stablecoins is still uncertain, they
promise to remain in the spotlight in 2027. We show that despite
multiple risks identiﬁed, European banks are unlikely to surrender

ground to ﬁntech stablecoin issuers without a ﬁght.
What are stablecoins?
Stablecoins are crypto assets issued by private institutions that
promise a stable nominal value in a given currency, commodity or
pool of assets. Stablecoins are therefore not issued (or backed) by a
government or central authority, but rather by a private institution
and secured by segregated reserves.
The crypto asset is issued on distributed ledger technology (DLT),
such as a blockchain, with three existing types of pegging

mechanisms. For the sake of this analysis, we will focus on the
main one: ﬁat-collateralised stablecoins. These crypto assets are
backed 1:1 by reserves of cash or liquid assets.
In our overview publication on stablecoins (read more here), we
review the sector’s recent development. We show that the total
market capitalisation of stablecoins is surpassing $300bn globally,
99% of which consists of USD-pegged tokens. While still dwarfed
by, for example, bank deposits, the rapid growth of the sector over

the past ﬁve years (up by US$250bn) has drawn a lot of attention.

over time & share of all cryptocurrencies

Growing interest in stablecoins stems both from possible eﬃciency
gains and the potential risks they pose. For stablecoins to reach the
‘broad adoption’ level of development, usage should extend
beyond the niche crypto landscape and into the payments world.
Several variables, including regulation, integration into payment
systems and relative attractiveness compared with other payment

systems, must be considered when determining the future growth
of stablecoins. Read more on that in our piece Stablecoins 101:
Uncertain growth but the stakes are high.
Despite their uncertain future, we believe it's important to consider
a scenario in which stablecoins become broadly adopted and
systemically important as a means of payment. We identify
multiple potential impacts of the crypto asset, including
implications for emerging markets, interest rates, and monetary

policy. In this piece, we will focus speciﬁcally on potential
implications for the banking sector.
Stablecoins impact on banks: Threat,
opportunity, or both?
In this section, we explore the potential implications of a broad use
of stablecoins beyond the crypto landscape and into payment
systems for banks. Despite US dollar stablecoins currently
dominating the market, our main assumption is the widespread
adoption of euro-denominated stablecoins in Europe.

### Page 4

While stablecoins are often presented as a challenge for the
banking sector, we review here ﬁve potential implications, not all of
which are negative for ﬁnancial institutions.
A change in banks' funding
To discuss the consequences of an uptake in stablecoin adoption
on banks’ funding, two variables must be considered:
1. Which assets are used to buy stablecoins
2. What type of reserves are required by the regulation
If most users acquire stablecoins by redeeming liquid investments,

the impact for ﬁnancial institutions is expected to remain limited.
However, if EU citizens and corporates adopt EUR-denominated
stablecoins using bank deposits, the impact on ﬁnancial
institutions could be substantially more signiﬁcant. In this scenario,
banks could face a net drop in their retail and corporate deposits
and/or a shift in the composition of their funding base.
Importantly, the nature of banks’ funding change depends on the
second key variable: the regulation in place, more speciﬁcally the

reserve regime. Indeed, widespread adoption of the crypto asset
with deposits could result in a drop in banks’ funding. However,
under the EU’s MiCAR, stablecoin issuers are required to hold part of
their reserves as deposits with European commercial banks.
This implies that part of the amount ﬂowing out of banks would
ultimately come back to the sector as reserves from stablecoin
issuers. Stablecoin adoption would therefore not translate into a
net drop in funding for banks but rather in a shift in their funding

base, where retail and corporate deposits would come back in
wholesale form (more speciﬁcally as ﬁnancial institution deposits).
Illustration of banks' funding shift through
stablecoins adoption using retail deposits*

*stylized example using MiCAR for systemic issuers
Source: Source: ING
This shift from retail to wholesale deposits is not without
consequences. Wholesale deposits are known to be more volatile
than retail ones and therefore represent a less stable funding source
for banks. Additionally, capital requirements are higher for
wholesale deposits, with, for example, higher run-oﬀ rates in the
Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) and lower available stable funding

weights in the Net Stable Funding Ratio (NSFR). This reﬂects their
greater volatility and related risks, making them more expensive for
banks to hold.
Greater reliance on wholesale deposits could also create risk
management challenges with more complex asset-liability
matching. The ease with which deposits can be switched into
stablecoins could increase ﬁnancial institutions’ exposure to
economic cycles.
To date, all main regulations in place ban stablecoin issuers from

directly remunerating holders. Therefore, an environment in which
interest rates are lowered (or even approach zero) would make
holding stablecoins more attractive for users, as the diﬀerence with
holding a deposit account is reduced. This could trigger sudden
stablecoin adoption and a quick shift in banks’ funding.
The opposite scenario could also pose challenges for banks. As
interest rates increase, the attractiveness of holding stablecoins
diminishes. This could lead to sudden redemption requests from

### Page 6

stablecoin holders, forcing issuers to liquidate reserves held at
banks. As a result, ﬁnancial institutions could face an outﬂow of
stablecoin issuers’ deposits with a simultaneous inﬂow of retail
deposits, adding volatility to their funding proﬁles.
In either case, these funding changes and substitution dynamics
could make banks’ funding management more complex, costly
and diﬃcult to predict. However, as long as regulation prevents
stablecoin issuers from directly remunerating holders, the eﬀect of

economic cycles is expected to remain muted, as stablecoins only
become attractive as a deposit-like instrument in the case of a very
low interest rate environment.
Additionally, the impact is likely to vary across the banking sector.
Financial institutions with a large base of digitally active customers
could face heightened pressure from widespread stablecoin
adoption, as the barriers to switching from deposits to crypto
assets are lower. Diﬀerences could also emerge between deposit

categories. The impact is expected to be especially noticeable for
accounts used as payment instruments, held by younger, digitally
native customers who tend to be less relationship-driven or loyal to
a speciﬁc bank and generally more willing to adopt new ﬁnancial
technologies.
While these points explore a scenario in which EUR-denominated
stablecoins are widespread, it’s important to note that the impact
would be diﬀerent if USD-denominated stablecoins continue to

dominate the market. In that case, a signiﬁcant uptake would not
translate into a funding shift for European banks but rather into a
net outﬂow of deposits.
Indeed, these funds could ﬂow to US stablecoin issuers, which
would place the safe assets as required under US regulation. In
other words, this would translate into a net outﬂow of European
deposits towards the US economy. Aside from the monetary policy
impact, European banks would be required to turn to alternative

and more expensive sources of funding to compensate for the
outﬂow of retail deposits.

### Page 7

However, EU banks can also play a central role in the issuance and
adoption of EUR-denominated stablecoins to counter that eﬀect, as
we discuss below.
Pressure on credit provision
In addition to changing banks’ funding base, broad stablecoin
adoption could also negatively impact credit provision through
several channels.
In a scenario where banks see a funding shift towards greater
reliance on wholesale deposits, higher liquidity requirements may

push them to take more conservative risk decisions. Research has
shown that this could translate into banks steering their balance
sheets away from illiquid and long-maturity loans to ensure they
comply with the regulation, as well as reducing loan volumes,
shortening maturities and increasing loan pricing. Overall, the
European Central Bank has shown that deposit substitution could
lead to a contraction of about 0.2% in lending to ﬁrms.
In addition to regulatory restrictions, wholesale deposits show

diﬀerent volatility patterns from those of retail deposits, mainly by
being more reactive to market and macroeconomic changes.
Greater reliance on such deposits would, once again, imply more
complex risk management, pushing banks to hold additional
liquidity buﬀers which could increase loan pricing.
Here, again, the impact could diﬀer depending on the ﬁnancial
institution. European banks already showing high loan-to-deposit
ratios, as well as those close to regulatory thresholds, could be

more inclined to pass higher funding costs on through lending
conditions in order to remain within regulatory thresholds.
Concentration and spillover risks
A growing discussion among regulators centres on concentration
risks. As stablecoin issuers are required to hold part of their reserves
as deposits with commercial banks, ﬁnancial institutions could
increasingly rely on their deposits for funding. As the proportion of
banks’ funding coming from a small number of stablecoin issuers

increases, so does concentration risk. This impact may become

### Page 8

substantial if stablecoin adoption is both signiﬁcant and
concentrated among a handful of issuers.
Concentration could also arise if stablecoin issuers maintain
relationships with only a limited number of banks, those with both
the technical capabilities and willingness to engage with the sector.
This could create pockets of concentration on both sides of the
business, introducing new systemic risks for the banking sector.
Regulators across the globe have been increasingly attentive to the

development of stablecoins. However, as concentration risks
between banks and stablecoin issuers potentially emerge, they
might also see the need to update their frameworks to ensure that
such risks are correctly accounted for and captured in stress testing.
In addition, the current EU regulation also creates a direct link
between the banking sector and crypto asset issuers, increasing
spillover risks. In the event of a run on stablecoins, where the

crypto asset suddenly loses value and users redeem their holdings,
banks carrying the issuer’s reserves could face sudden and
signiﬁcant redemption requests. This could transmit a shock in the
crypto sector to the broader ﬁnancial system. The reverse is also
true, with stablecoin issuers potentially aﬀected by the failure of, or
reputational damage to, the commercial bank they hold reserves
with.
These concerns promise to remain relevant in 2027 as the EU is

reviewing its MiCA Regulation. In May 2026, the European
Commission opened a consultation to assess whether the MiCAR
remains ‘ﬁt for purpose’ following its initial implementation and
recent market developments. In September, the ECB, alongside the
27 EU national central banks, submitted a recommendation to drop
the requirement for stablecoin issuers to hold deposits at
commercial banks.
Instead, the European System of Central Banks proposes
implementing a minimum percentage of assets maturing within

one to ﬁve working days to be held by the stablecoin issuer. This is
said to lower both concentration and spillover risks. However, it

### Page 9

could further accentuate the impact on banks’ funding by
transforming a funding shift into a net deposit outﬂow.
Stablecoin and, more generally, crypto asset regulations are still
emerging globally. Other regulators have highlighted the risks that
links between stablecoins and the banking sector could create. The
most striking example comes from the UK, where the Bank of
England announced that it aims to ban stablecoin issuers from
holding reserves at commercial banks, speciﬁcally to mitigate

spillover risks in case of a shock. As stablecoin usage continues to
rise, we can expect further changes to the regulatory landscape
globally.
Competing through innovation
Despite the lack of international harmonisation, current regulations
restrict stablecoin issuers from directly distributing remuneration to
holders. This represents a signiﬁcant regulatory advantage for the
banking sector, as it limits the use of the crypto asset to
transactional purposes. Remuneration distribution is therefore a

central discussion point for stablecoin issuers, as removing the ban
would allow them to position their product not only as a means of
payment but also as a store of value. 2027 will be a crucial year as
negotiations surrounding the US Clarity Act continue, potentially
allowing stablecoin issuers to directly distribute rewards. While still
far from an agreement, such a derogation would be a ﬁrst globally
and could open the door for similar changes in other jurisdictions.

To counter this competition, banks could increase interest rates on
their deposit accounts; however, this would have an impact on their
proﬁtability.
Therefore, the critical response to the rise of stablecoins lies
instead in innovation. Indeed, crypto assets’ main advantage lies
in their speed and limited costs, but banks are already showing
they can innovate to compete. The European payment landscape
is relatively cheap and fast despite being heavily fragmented. Some

national payment systems exist, but a large share of transactions
are processed through non-EU providers. Stablecoins’ promise to
process payments (nearly) instantly and at extremely low costs,

### Page 10

even across borders, could put pressure on existing systems.
However, banks and payment system providers are already
exploring ways to compete and experimenting with new digital
transaction methods.
One example lies in tokenisation. Tokenised assets are digital
representations of real-world assets on a distributed ledger
infrastructure. A wide range of assets can be tokenised, but most
banks are experimenting with deposit tokenisation. These could

modernise payment and settlement infrastructures as well as
intragroup liquidity management. They promise faster settlement,
24/7 transactions, programmability and lower operational frictions.
Additionally, tokenised deposits oﬀer regulatory advantages, as
they remain fully within the existing supervisory framework. The
underlying assets stay on the commercial banks’ balance sheet,
preserving the same oversight and prudential standards applied to

traditional deposits.
Banks’ competitive response could also extend to the regulatory
ﬁeld. The sector could push regulators to implement or maintain
strict rules for the crypto asset sector to adequately reﬂect their
risks. The US provides us with a striking example, as negotiations
for the Clarity Act have been ongoing for months, with ﬁnancial
institutions pressing the importance of the ‘same risk, same
regulation’ principle. While the ﬁnal version of the US crypto

landscape regulation is still to be agreed upon, US banks have
played a central role in the negotiations by requesting
proportionate safeguards surrounding the use of crypto assets.
Banks' role in stablecoin adoption
While we have explored in depth the impact stablecoins could have
on the banking sector upon broad adoption, it’s important to
highlight that banks also play a role in the crypto asset’s
adoption. A central variable in determining the future adoption and

development of stablecoins is the degree to which they are
integrated into existing payment systems. Greater integration not
only increases accessibility for a broader range of customers but
also improves the ease of use. This is especially relevant as

### Page 11

stablecoins are still mainly used in the crypto landscape and by
digitally savvy users.
Banks could therefore support the adoption of stablecoins for
retail, day-to-day payments by integrating the asset into their
digital systems or creating their own tokens, adding it as a new
payment rail. Several examples in the EU and the US show the role
banks can play in issuing their own stablecoins, defending their
place in the payment landscape, even when it comes to new

technologies. By issuing their own stablecoins, banks could also
avoid losing client relationships and reduce the risk of becoming
commodity providers for new payment issuers. Beyond this, banks
are also exploring ways in which stablecoin integration could
improve the customer experience while increasing fee revenues.
Looking speciﬁcally at Europe, banks’ stablecoin issuance could
also play a signiﬁcant role in the growth of EUR-denominated
stablecoins. To date, USD-pegged stablecoins represent a striking

99% of all stablecoins in use. The widespread use of USD
stablecoins in the EU would have signiﬁcant monetary implications
on top of those discussed here for the banking sector. Over the past
few years, the European Union has increasingly focused on its
‘strategic autonomy’ agenda. Part of the discussion concerns the
Union’s signiﬁcant reliance on non-EU payment services, primarily
US-based ones. The development of EUR-denominated stablecoins
by local banks could provide an answer to these concerns, allowing

for the development of a fast, cost-eﬃcient European payment
system directly competing with non-EU alternatives.
Potentially signiﬁcant implications, but
banks are unlikely to surrender ground to
ﬁntech stablecoin issuers
The broad adoption of stablecoins could have a signiﬁcant impact
on European banks. These range from a net drop in retail deposits
or a shift towards wholesale funding to greater concentration and
spillover risks, as well as an overall negative eﬀect on credit

provision and potentially banks’ business models.

### Page 12

Fintech Cryptocurrency Banks
While it remains important to continue exploring these risks as
stablecoin use grows, it’s also crucial to highlight that the banking
sector has tools at its disposal to not only counter them, but also
beneﬁt from the underlying technology. Indeed, one should not
expect the “traditional” ﬁnancial sector to simply stand aside as
crypto assets continue to gain traction.
Despite signiﬁcant growth over the past ﬁve years, stablecoins

remain far from reaching widespread adoption. In our previous
publication (read more here), we identiﬁed several factors that
could shape their future. For banks, the key question is how to
compete with the beneﬁts stablecoins oﬀer. The sector is already
making substantial progress in instant-payment technologies and
exploring digital alternatives (such as tokenisation) that could
provide similar beneﬁts with fewer regulatory concerns. These
initiatives could signiﬁcantly reduce stablecoins’ relative appeal.

Furthermore, it’s essential to remember that banks still play a
central role in payments and could also play a crucial role in the
uptake of stablecoins by issuing their own tokens. This includes
inﬂuencing which currency-pegged stablecoins gain traction and
how they are integrated into the broader ﬁnancial system.
Next year promises to deliver another wave of stablecoin-related
news. In the EU, the MiCAR review could translate into a change in
the reserve framework, a key issue when assessing the potential

impact of stablecoins on banks. In the UK, 2027 will also be an
important year as it will see the enforcement of its comprehensive
stablecoin regime in October. The US must also still ﬁnalise its
CLARITY Act, potentially allowing stablecoin issuers to directly
distribute remuneration to holders. This would be a world ﬁrst and
a major shift in the regulatory treatment of the crypto asset,
opening the door to similar changes in other countries.
While the upcoming regulatory changes could alter the potential

impact of stablecoin adoption on the banking sector, we should not
expect banks to surrender ground to ﬁntech stablecoin issuers
without a ﬁght.

Document 405: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

includes larger-than-usual JGB issuance. 
• Japan real wages, which have maintained positive yoy

• Japan consumer sentiment, which declined in September.
